**Where to look:** what to do next → [Next up](#next-up--recommended-todos) · everything still open →
[All open work](#all-open-work) · how to maintain this file → [Keeping this file up to date](#keeping-this-file-up-to-date) ·
how far along we are → [Status summary](#status-summary).

Written by `TODO/docs-003-roadmap.md`, per `CLAUDE.md`'s Working Files section: built
from `core-001`/`core-002`'s real implementation experience (not just
`Initial design.md`'s plan) and `ui-001`'s confirmed per-control interaction-model
answers — both are now done, see their retirement notes in `TODO/done/` for what
actually shipped versus what was originally scoped. `docs-003` itself turned out to
have an undeclared prerequisite chain (`build-001` was needed before `core-001` could
run at all); see `TODO/done/build-001-project-scaffold.md`'s own note.

## Keeping this file up to date

This file has three jobs, and they go stale at different speeds. Update the fast ones every time:

| Part | Goes stale when | Who updates it |
|---|---|---|
| **Milestone tables** (✅ prefix) and **Completion Log** | a task finishes | `/fix-todo` Step 8, in the same commit as the task — already automatic |
| **Next up** and **All open work** | a task finishes, opens, or changes priority; a dependency lands | whoever closes or adds the task, same commit |
| **Status summary** | a milestone's count or state changes | same commit as the row that changed it |

**When a task finishes**

1. `/fix-todo` already prefixes the row with ✅, appends the Completion Log row, and moves the file to
   `TODO/done/`. Do not skip any of the three.
2. In **All open work**, delete the task's row.
3. In **Next up**, delete it if it was listed. Then find what it unblocked: any open file whose `depends:`
   ids all have a file in `TODO/done/` is now **ready**. Change its State cell to `▶ ready`, and give it a
   row in Next up if it outranks what is there.
4. In **Status summary**, bump the milestone's count. When the last row of a milestone closes, change its
   icon to ✅ and, per `CLAUDE.md` Verification, note that the free-form human pass is due.

**When a task is added or re-prioritised**

1. Write `TODO/<id>.md` with the usual front matter (`priority`, `milestone`, `depends`, `status`, `model`).
2. Add a row to its milestone table — or to **Findings** if it has none. A file with no row is invisible
   to `/run-todos`; six were found in that state on 2026-10-12.
3. Add or edit its row in **All open work**, and re-rank Next up if the priority changed.

**Rules for the summary rows**

- One line, under ~200 characters. Counts, state, and what is left — never the story of how it was built.
  That belongs in the task's own retirement note and its Completion Log row.
- Counts are derived from the milestone tables, not remembered. Count the ✅ rows.
- Bump the **Last reviewed** date in Next up whenever you re-rank it.

**Spot check** (finds open files that have no row in this file; should print nothing)

```sh
for f in TODO/[a-z]*-[0-9]*.md; do id=$(basename "$f" | grep -oE '^[a-z]+-[0-9]+[a-z]?'); \
  grep -q "\`$id\`" TODO/ROADMAP.md || echo "missing from ROADMAP: $id"; done
```

## How to read this file

- Each milestone is a table. A row is either a **real task** (`✅`/plain id links to a
  written `TODO/<id>.md`) or a **reserved id** (no file yet — named here so dependency
  order and numbering are fixed, written as its own `/fix-todo`-sized task when a
  milestone is actually being worked). Both count toward `CLAUDE.md`'s "every
  UI-touching catalog row becomes its own reachability-gated task" rule — a reserved
  row is a placeholder for a future *individual* task, not a batch.
- **Kind** marks Creation vs. Operation per `CLAUDE.md`'s non-negotiable split — never
  mixed in one control, never one dropdown.
- **Acceptance** names which `Initial design.md` §9 / future `TODO/ACCEPTANCE.md`
  scenario (numbered 1-8, per §9) the row plausibly affects, so `docs-004` and every
  later `/fix-todo` run know what to re-drive.

## A granularity decision this file had to make, stated explicitly

`CLAUDE.md` says every §6 catalog row becomes its own reachability-gated task, "not a
deferred batch." Taken completely literally, that would mean seven near-identical
tasks for Cube/Cylinder/Cone/Hexagon/Triangle/Tube/Sphere, each wiring the *same* drag-
to-place mechanism to a different registry op. That reads as fragmentation for its own
sake, not the failure mode the rule exists to prevent (a boolean operation hidden in a
flat feature-registry dropdown, per `PREVIOUS-ATTEMPT.md`). This file instead groups
by **distinct interaction pattern**, not by literal table row: one task wires the
generic "drag-place a primitive" mechanism to all seven base primitives at once (one
`*.e2e.js` spec exercising two of them is enough to prove the *mechanism* is reachable,
which is what the reachability rule is actually protecting), while hardware items,
kit-parts, and Raspberry Pi accessories — which differ from each other in real ways
(snap guides, composite recipes with their own size tables, per-item health risks like
the RPi board's snap-before-measurement problem below) — do get their own rows. This
interpretation is a judgment call, not a re-litigation of the rule; flag it for
revision if a milestone turns out to need finer granularity once it's actually worked.

## Kickoff decisions this roadmap assumes as resolved

- **Geometry kernel: Committed** — `@jscad/modeling` (JSCAD V3). See
  `docs/decisions/geometry-kernel.md` (`docs-001`).
- **Document model / sync: Committed (accepted 2026-10-12)** — a hand-rolled, state-based
  merge over the existing NDJSON project file (replica ids, Lamport stamps, position keys,
  tombstones; record lines byte-identical; merge metadata in the state line, version 2), **no
  CRDT library**. See `docs/decisions/document-sync.md` (`docs-022`, 2026-10-12), which
  supersedes `docs-002`'s single-writer deferral and keeps it as history. Accepted by the user
  2026-10-12, which unblocks `data-008` and the rest of M15. Until M15 lands, the shipped app is
  still the single-writer log `docs-002` described.
- **Kernel-adapter pattern: proven** — `core-001` confirmed the composite/native
  split and the V1→V3 Group port against real code, not just source-reading. Its
  retirement note flags one real limit carried into M4 below (a composite recipe
  can't call `group.snap` against geometry it didn't create itself — see M4's note).
- **Replay engine: built and hardened** — `core-002` built the NDJSON + fold engine
  and, in the process, fixed a real pre-existing bug in `core-001`'s registry-override
  path. `Initial design.md` §9 scenario 8 ("remote-load safety") already has a passing
  test against the real kernel (`src/core/replay.spec.js`) — `docs-004` should record
  this scenario as already-passing when it transcribes §9, not merely drafted.

## M1 — Viewport scaffold + first primitive creation

[↑ Contents](#contents)

Everything below this line needs a viewport to place geometry into — this whole
milestone is `Initial design.md` §8 step 8 and §10's "ships with the first `*.e2e.js`
spec, at all three viewports and all three engines, because it's WebGL from line one."

| id | Task | Kind | Depends | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ `render-001` | App shell + viewport scaffold (grid, orientation gizmo, nav chrome) | — | `build-001` | ✅ done — **three.js confirmed** as the renderer (`docs/decisions/rendering.md`, compared against Babylon.js/PlayCanvas on real health metrics; Stack row moved to Committed, pinned to `0.185.1` since three.js has no semver). `AppView.vue` is now the real mounted root. Ships the first `*.e2e.js`, running the full 3 viewport × 3 engine matrix. §9 scenario 7 (touch-only nav) **driven by hand and PASS** — that drive caught a light-mode defect (dark scene under light chrome) no test could see; fixed here |
| ✅ `ui-010` | Drag-place a primitive — generic mechanism wired to all 7 base primitives (Cube/Cuboid, Cylinder, Cone, Hexagon, Triangle, Tube, Sphere) | Creation | `render-001` | ✅ done — **§9 scenario 1 driven by hand and PASS** (laptop/Chromium and phone/WebKit). One drag mechanism parameterised by the row's `op`, not seven copies: rows come from the registry's own new `ui` metadata via `src/core/library.js`. Grew two required additions the spec called out up front: the **command layer** (`src/core/document.js` — origin-tagged append log folding incrementally through `core-002`'s `applyAction`, plus thin `src/stores/document.js`/`selection.js` wrappers), and the **4 missing kernel primitives** (`cone`, `triangle`, `tube`, `sphere`; `triangle` needed extruding because upstream's is 2D, `cone`/`tube` compose like `hexagon` already did). Also added the mesh bridge (`src/render/mesh.js`) and working-plane projection (`src/render/pick.js`) nothing had built yet. The hand-drive caught a phone-width defect no test saw — the Library sheet's tab covering the nav chrome — fixed here |
| ✅ `ui-011` | Selection → inspector + dimension lines + gizmo (display only) | — (selection display) | `ui-010` | ✅ done — **§9 scenarios 2 and 6 driven by hand, both PARTIAL PASS** (laptop/Chromium, tablet/WebKit, phone/WebKit), with the undrivable halves named rather than glossed: scenario 2's Inspector-opens-with-fields half passes with a Cylinder standing in for the absent Bolt row, while its property-edit and gizmo-drag steps need `ui-013`/`ui-012`; scenario 6's single-item and 2-selected contracts pass, while group/sub-part pick depth needs `ui-015`/`ui-016`. Scenario 1 re-checked and its previously-deferred "gizmo and dimension lines attach" half now **met**. Screen-space SVG overlays, matching `render-001`'s established pattern rather than a second overlay technology; all the maths lives framework-free in `src/render/annotate.js` (world→screen, asserted to be the exact inverse of `pick.js`) and `src/core/inspector.js` (fields read from the registry's own `ui.defaults`, so an eighth primitive gets a correct Inspector with no component change). Added `src/core/units.js` as the single mm/degree display boundary, and the minimum shift-click multi-select needed to reach control 3's 2+ state. **The hand-drive caught two phone-width defects no test saw** — the Inspector and Library sheets stacking into unreadable mush, and the nav chrome/scale readout sitting behind the sheet's top edge — both fixed here |
| ✅ `ui-012` | Gizmo move/rotate ↔ numeric-field sync per `docs/decisions/interaction-model-qa.md` control 4 | — | `ui-011` | ✅ done — **§9 scenario 2 re-driven and its whole gizmo/field half now PASSES**, scenario 1 re-checked and PASS (laptop/Chromium, tablet/WebKit, phone/WebKit). The task's real content was a **document-layer supersession mechanism**, not gizmo wiring: a transform is an appended record with its own id, so the naive `bodies()` would have rendered the moved body *and* the one it moved from — doubling it on the first drag, the inverse of scenario 1's regression. Chose a **`supersedes` field on the record plus a lineage index** (`rootById` / `terminalByRoot`) that `src/core/document.js` maintains, over a mutable latest-id pointer or deleting the superseded entry, precisely because it **deletes nothing** — every intermediate record stays in `entries` and `scene.byId` for the undo/redo a later task needs; supersession changes only what gets double-rendered. A `Body` now reports its **lineage root** as `id` (so a selection and a three.js mesh survive a move) and its **terminal** record as `recordId` (whose geometry renders), with `op`/`params`/`name` still from the root — which is why `src/core/inspector.js` needed no change to keep showing "a Cube with a 40 mm size" after it moves. Both triggers go through one framework-free `src/core/transform.js`, so a drag and a typed field cannot drift. Also added the `transform.rotate` registry op (degrees in, radians at the registry boundary, turning about the body's own centre rather than the world origin), a live-preview store so the fields track a drag without appending a record per frame, and fatter invisible hit targets for fingertips. **Group selection is explicitly deferred** to whichever task lands group-combine (`ui-015`, M3) rather than guessed at |
| ✅ `ui-017` | Transform gizmo scales to the selection's bounding box, instead of a fixed pixel size | — (gizmo sizing) | `ui-012` | ✅ done — **scenario 2's gizmo-drag half re-driven (still PARTIAL PASS for its own, unrelated, unchanged reasons) and scenario 1's gizmo-attaches half re-checked, both PASS**. New `gizmoSizeForBounds` helper in `src/render/annotate.js` sizes the arm/ring from the selected body's own `boundsMm` half-diagonal converted via `mmPerPixel`, floored at the old fixed 58px/40px (`ui-012`'s tuned grabbable minimum) and capped so an enormous body can't run the gizmo off screen; `Viewport.vue`'s `syncAnnotations()` now computes this per frame instead of using the two constants. `Gizmo.vue` needed no change — its hit targets already derive from the same `arrow.tipX/tipY`/ring points. Found and fixed a real regression in the process: a pre-existing `ui-012` e2e spec pressed the arm's rendered *midpoint*, which the now-longer arm can legitimately extend past — updated to grab near the hub end, which stays inside the body's clear zone by design regardless of arm length. No custom-size primitive placement exists in the UI yet, so both the unit tests and the e2e addition vary on-screen size via zoom instead of a literal large/small pair — see the retired TODO's own note |
| ✅ `ui-018` | Shift+left-drag or right-drag pans the viewport, without switching the nav-chrome mode | — (camera nav) | `render-001` | ✅ done — **scenario 7 re-driven and PASS, scenario 1's click/select half re-checked and PASS**. `Viewport.vue`'s pointer handlers now decide an *effective* per-gesture drag mode: right mouse button always pans; Shift+left pans only once the press's travel exceeds the existing `CLICK_SLOP_PX` threshold, reusing the same click-vs-drag distinction `render-001`'s selection click already relied on rather than a second one that could disagree with it. The button/Shift state is captured once at `pointerdown` (mirroring `pressId`/`pressTravelPx`) so releasing Shift mid-drag can't flip the gesture back to orbit. Added `@contextmenu.prevent` on the canvas so a right-drag's release doesn't pop the native menu — confirmed suppressed by dispatching a real `contextmenu` event in the hand-drive, not just by reading the template. The existing Shift-click additive-select path at the old line 754 is untouched and re-verified with a new regression spec |
| ✅ `ui-019` | Configurable mouse-button mapping for pan/orbit, persisted in `localStorage` | — (camera nav, settings) | `ui-018` | ✅ done — **scenario 7 re-driven and PASS** (hand-driven at the iPad viewport, WebKit, touch-only). New `src/stores/settings.js` — the project's first `localStorage` use — holds a `mouseNavPreset` id (`'shift-or-right-pans'` default, `'left-pans'` alternate), read once behind a try/catch so a blocked/throwing store can never crash startup, written back on every change. `Viewport.vue`'s `ui-018` `effectiveDragMode()` was split into a small `mouseDragOverride(preset)` mapping table plus a `pressPointerType === 'mouse'` gate — added because a touch `PointerEvent` also reports `button: 0`, and without the gate the `left-pans` preset would start panning on an ordinary one-finger touch orbit; confirmed by both a new e2e regression test and the hand-drive. `NavigationChrome.vue` gets a gear icon opening an upward popover (so it can never reach the top-right orientation gizmo) listing the two presets, marking the active one; it reads/writes the settings store directly rather than round-tripping through `Viewport.vue`. This completes **M1** |

## M2 — Hardware + kit-part catalog, snap-guide placement

[↑ Contents](#contents)

| id | Task | Kind | Depends | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ `ui-013` | Drag-place hardware/kit-part with snap guides — generic snap-assist mechanism | Creation | `ui-010` | ✅ done — **§9 scenario 3 driven by hand and PASS** (laptop/Chromium, phone/WebKit, tablet/WebKit), with a **Cube and Cylinder standing in** for the M4 RPi-plus-camera pair per the task's own sequencing note; scenario 1 re-driven in full and PASS, because this task changes the drop-commit path itself. The snap is one mechanism over any two placeable items: five candidate alignments per working-plane axis (centre, two flush faces, two touching faces) scored by how far each would move the ghost, nearest wins within a **screen-pixel** tolerance converted at the boundary (`SNAP_TOLERANCE_PX` → `snapToleranceMm`), so it reads the same at any zoom. `group.js` was **not** touched: `calcSnap`'s arithmetic is reused as scalars (`alignmentOffsetMm`) because it allocates a vector and measures a bounding box per call and this runs on every `pointermove` — with the equivalence **asserted against `calcSnap`/`calcCenterWith` themselves** rather than claimed. Added a **proximity gate** the TODO didn't anticipate (an axis snaps only when the ghost is also within tolerance on the *other* axis), without which a drop at the far corner of the grid was silently pulled into line with something 400 mm away. The hand-drive caught **two defects no test could see**: a centred guide spanned only the two coincident boxes, making it a dashed stub exactly as long as the solid it lay on (now overhangs by one tolerance each end), and the guides shared `--accent` with the gizmo's rings and the Z dimension line so they read as part of the gizmo (now their own `--snap-guide` token, both colour schemes). No Hardware Library section was added — that stays with `core-010`/`core-011`/`core-012` |
| ✅ `core-010` | `hardware.nut` composite recipe, dimensionally diffed against V1 | Creation (op) | `core-001` | ✅ done — single-record `primitive.hexagon` expansion (`diameter = acrossCornersMm + FIT_CLEARANCE_MM[fit]`), dimensionally diffed against real `Hardware.Nut()` V1 output for `m4`/loose, `m4`/tap (the one negative-delta fit class) and `1/4 hex`/close, all within tolerance on size and volume — not raw bounds, because V3 centres the hexagon (matching every other `primitive.*` row `library.js` places) where V1 built it base-at-z=0, a deliberate divergence documented in the recipe's own module doc. `fit` has no default (V1's own `Hardware.Nut` has none either, unlike `Bolt`'s `loose`) — an omitted or unknown fit throws the same way an unknown `spec` does. New shared `tables/fit-clearances.js` (`FIT_CLEARANCE_MM`) lands here for `core-011`'s washer recipe to reuse. **Confirmed the Library panel is *not* purely registry-driven**: `src/core/library.js`'s `buildRows` filters registry keys to `op.startsWith('primitive.')`, so a registered `hardware.nut` (or `hardware.bolt`) op is invisible to the panel regardless of `ui` metadata until an explicit Hardware Library section reads composite ops too — confirming the note `ui-013` left behind. No acceptance scenario moves on this task alone (no UI surface yet); no closed finding's subsystem needed re-running (`core-001`, the only prior task touching this composite-recipe machinery, is a feature not a finding) |
| ✅ `core-011` | `hardware.washer` composite recipe, dimensionally diffed against V1 | Creation (op) | `core-001` | ✅ done — one-or-two-record expansion (`primitive.tube` for `washer`, always; `primitive.cylinder` for `clearance`, `hidden: true`, only when `params.fit` is given), dimensionally diffed against real `Hardware.Washer()` V1 output for `ImperialWashers['1/4']` with `fit: 'loose'` and with `fit` omitted — both branches of the recipe's one real conditional — within tolerance on size and volume, not raw bounds, same centred-vs-base-at-z=0 divergence `core-010` already documented. `clearance` is marked `hidden: true`, diverging from V1's visible-red-part default, mirroring `core-001`'s `hardware.bolt` `tap-*` precedent for the same reason (scenario 5 needs the real hole size). **`MetricWashers['m3'..'m12']` has no V1 source at any version** (confirmed against the vendored 1.1.0, the author's own current 5.0.0 tree, and `jscad-hardware2`'s v1 tree — none ever shipped a washer table for metric) — ported from the published ISO 7089 standard instead, verified by a table-conformance test against the TODO's own values rather than a V1 diff, plus an `m6` recipe-shape check (both branches) proving metric and imperial specs expand identically. Reused `core-010`'s `tables/fit-clearances.js` (`FIT_CLEARANCE_MM`) rather than recreating it. No acceptance scenario moves on this task alone (no UI surface; `hardware.washer` stays unreachable from the Library panel per `core-010`'s already-confirmed `buildRows` filter to `op.startsWith('primitive.')`); no closed finding's subsystem needed re-running (`core-001`/`core-010`, the only prior tasks touching this composite-recipe machinery, are features, not findings) |
| ✅ `core-012` | Kit-part composite recipes: Board, BoardFrame, Anchor (§6's multi-part-`Group`-as-one-draggable example) — `BoardFrame`/`Anchor` don't exist in the `jscad-utils@3.2.0` vendored here, but are confirmed in the user's `jscad-utils@6.0.0`; all three fully scoped, ported by reading that source (no fixture-diff dependency added) | Creation (op) | `core-001` | ✅ done — all three landed as `composite(0, ...)`, and the task needed **one thing its own Affected-files list didn't have**: a 2D vocabulary. A composite expands into *other registry ops*, and there were none for a profile or an extrusion — `primitive.triangle` had hidden that shape inside a single native entry, which cannot work for `BoardFrame`, whose whole point is a `boolean.subtract` sitting **between** the profile and the extrude (V1's own comment: 2D subtraction first, to avoid the BSP non-manifold artifacts of subtracting two same-height slabs). So two native ops came with it — **`profile.roundedRectangle`** and **`profile.extrudeLinear`** — deliberately *not* named `primitive.*`, because `library.js` filters the Library panel on that prefix and **throws** on a match without `ui` metadata, so a `primitive.roundedRectangle` would either break the panel or offer a flat profile as a draggable body (pinned by a `registry.spec.js` test; the record ordering is pinned too, so a refactor can't quietly invert it). `boolean.subtract` needed no change — it dispatches on its arguments' own geometry type, and the fold's `toGeom3` leaves a non-`Group` value untouched. `kit.anchor` is the **first recipe in the project to touch `@jscad/modeling`**, and only to build throwaway probe geometry: `align`/`snap` are defined over bounding boxes and a tessellated cylinder's box depends on its segment count, so unlike `hardware.bolt`'s spec-table arithmetic there is no number to compute analytically — it measures with `group.js`'s `calcCenterWith`/`calcSnap`, discards the probes, and bakes the vector into one `transform.translate` (the spec re-derives it from the real bounding boxes rather than trusting the constant). The flagged `rotateX(90)` sign check was done by measurement, and the answer is that the sign is **immaterial**: the post is symmetric about its own axis, so ±90° give bit-identical boxes and the align/snap fix the position outright. No V1 fixture for `BoardFrame`/`Anchor` (6.0.0 isn't installed and wasn't added), so these get geometric-relation assertions instead — the post's −Y face flush with the hole's wall, the wall volume as the difference of the two areas. **§9 scenario 1 re-driven and PASS** even though this task's own Testing section judged no scenario to move: adding five registry entries is a change to the pipeline `library.js` builds its rows from, so `ui-010`'s repro was re-run — exactly seven `primitive.*` rows still, no `profile.*`/`kit.*` leakage, and all three recipes evaluated through the real fold in the browser. **M2's last row — M2 is now fully ✅ and needs the human free-form pass before M3 starts.** |

The real content was again **document-layer**, not toolbar wiring: a boolean is 2-in/1-out, which `supersedes` cannot express — it *continues* one body's identity, and a Subtract result continues neither operand's. Driven exactly the case this task's own Testing section named: place two solids, group them (one body remains, gizmo/dimension lines size to the combined bounding box, each solid's own geometry intact underneath), then place a third unrelated body — body count came out correct and the group's own centre stayed byte-identical. Fixed with a container-relative `max-width` plus unconditional row-wrap, confirmed by direct cross-engine bounding-box measurement and re-driven by hand with real touch input |

Scenario 1 re-driven **in full** and PASS, because this changes how every Group-valued body becomes a mesh; scenario 2's Inspector-fields contract re-driven and PASS, because `describeSelection`'s signature changed under it. The file's own headline call was right: the real content was the **render path**, not the click handler — every consumer used to flatten a Group through `toGeom3`→`combine()`, a CSG union after which no polygon remembers which part it came from, so there was literally no data for a click handler to resolve. Now one `Mesh` per named part under a `THREE.Group`, plus a recursive raycast. Both param-recovery cases landed as specified: a `group.assemble` result recovers each part's *original* op/params through `consumes`×`params.names` — walking `supersedes` back to the creation record, so a part that was **moved before grouping** still shows its own size rather than the translate that moved it — while a composite recipe's parts fall back to name + measurement. The gizmo is genuinely **absent** while drilled in, not inert, and the Transform fields go read-only, since no record exists that a `transform.*` could target meaning "just this part". One defect the passing specs could not see and the drive did: pick depth changes no body id, so nothing invalidated the render — the gizmo stayed on screen until the next camera move (fixed with a `subPart` watcher). ## M4+ — Raspberry Pi accessory catalog, sketch mode, flagged-risk follow-ups

[↑ Contents](#contents)

**`core-014a` is done and this milestone is unblocked — read
`docs/decisions/composite-recipe-geometry.md` before starting `core-014`.** `core-001`
flagged that a recipe emits records before any geometry exists, so it can't measure
geometry it didn't itself create, and named two candidate fixes (two-phase
expand→evaluate→measure→re-expand, or promoting `group.snap` to a real op record the
fold resolves) without picking one. **Neither has to be built as a general mechanism**:
`core-013` already shipped the second as a top-level op, and every real accessory
attachment in `jscad-raspberrypi` decomposes exactly into `core-012`'s origin probe
(internal offsets) plus a top-level `group.snap`/`group.align` record (placement) —
exact rather than approximate, because `calcSnap`/`calcCenterWith` are
translation-equivariant. Two-phase expansion is **not adopted**.

The decision doc carries one requirement that changes how `core-016`-`core-018` get
scoped, so don't skip it: eight of these accessory recipes take an externally-built
board group as a parameter, and the port has to **drop that parameter** and expand
standalone rather than try to resolve it against `core-020`'s empty fold.

| id | Task | Kind | Depends | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ `core-014a` | Decide how a composite recipe measures geometry it didn't create | — (architecture) | `core-001`'s flagged limitation | ✅ done — decision recorded in `docs/decisions/composite-recipe-geometry.md`: **two-phase expansion not adopted**, no new engineering, M4 unblocked. The roadmap's own instruction to "confirm by running the task before treating M4 as unblocked" earned its keep — **the scoping pass's survey was wrong on its central factual claim.** It concluded no real recipe attaches a separately-built accessory to a separately-built board; a fresh survey of the whole `jscad-raspberrypi` source (not just the camera-module subset it had sampled) found **eight recipes that do**, two of them requiring the board parameter (`touch-screen2.js:16`, `touch-display-7inch.js:16`). This project's own `core-013` fixture table already said so — 719 of 2,147 real call sites (~33%) name `.parts.` on the reference side. The decision survives on **different grounds**: the external board is used only to *position* the finished accessory, which is exactly `core-013`'s shipped top-level op, and the split is exact because `calcSnap`/`calcCenterWith` are translation-equivariant (`kit.anchor.js:142-145`). `spacer.js:52` (`mb = mb \|\| BPlus().parts.mb`) is V1 itself already using `core-012`'s probe pattern. | ✅ `core-021` | Angled `bisect` — the `angle`/`rotateaxis`/`rotateoffset` tilted-cutplane form V1 supports and `core-019` deliberately left out | Operation, 1 | `core-019` | ✅ done — and the roadmap line's own premise turned out to be wrong in the helpful direction: **no rotated cut-plane construction was needed at all.** The tilt is a coordinate-transform composition around `core-019`'s existing half-space-box cut (`cut(S, T·P) = T(cut(T⁻¹S, P))`), so `bisect` turns the solid by `−angle`, runs the *same* straight cut, and turns both halves back — no new boolean geometry. `resolveAxisPositionMm` and `cutHalfSpaces` factored out and shared by both paths; a falsy `angleDeg` short-circuits to the straight path with **zero** rotation calls, asserted by exact equality rather than closeness. Diffed against 10 new measured-V1 `bisectAngled` fixture cases, including both real call shapes from `core-019`'s survey. **The real find was a latent `core-019` defect this task's fixtures exposed: the straight port was wrong on the `y` axis.** V1's cut-plane basis is `['X','Z']` for y — a *left*-handed pair, normal `−y` — against right-handed `['Y','Z']`/`['X','Y']` for x/z, so V1's `negative` is the **max**-side half on `y` alone. `core-019`'s fixture covered only x and z, so nothing measured it and `group.spec.js` encoded the wrong expectation. Fixed in both paths via one `NEGATIVE_IS_MAX_SIDE` table, pinned by two new *straight* `y` fixture cases, and corrected in `docs/resize-bisect.md`, whose "`negative` (the near side)" line was now misleading. Second time on this same function that reading V1 gave the wrong answer and running it gave the right one. **Tolerance came out the opposite of the file's own prediction**: the two extra rotations were expected to need a looser comparison, but the worst residual across all ten angled cases is **4.5e-10 mm**, at the fixture's 9-decimal rounding floor — so the angled specs compare at the same 6 places the straight ones do. 848 unit tests green (+22); `e2e/resize-bisect-operation.e2e.js` re-run green on `chromium-laptop`, incl. its real Y-axis bisect drilled via `ui-016` pick-depth. No acceptance scenario driven, per this task's own "none directly" (kernel-only, no new UI surface) — scoped in `TODO/done/core-021-angled-bisect.md` |
| ✅ `core-022` | Bisect's `negative` half should always be the min-side piece, regardless of axis (diverge from V1's own `y`-axis quirk `core-021` faithfully reproduced) | Operation, 1 | `core-021` | ✅ done — a deletion, not new logic: `NEGATIVE_IS_MAX_SIDE` and its consulting branch in `asGroup` are gone, so `bisect` now unconditionally returns `{ negative: lower, positive: upper }` on every axis, straight and angled paths alike. The fixture (real, measured V1 output) is untouched by design; a new `expectedPartsFor(axis, fixtureParts)` helper in `group.spec.js` swaps which fixture-measured half `y`-axis cases compare against, so the regression coverage still proves the fixture is matched correctly under the new naming. New explicit contract test asserts `negative`'s bounds sit on the minimum side of `positive`'s, directly on geometry, for all three axes — the actual behavior this task adds, not just a fixture diff. `docs/resize-bisect.md`'s per-axis caveat paragraph is gone. 848 unit tests + typecheck green; `e2e/resize-bisect-operation.e2e.js` re-run on `chromium-laptop`, confirmed genuinely name-agnostic as the file claimed (6/6 green, no spec changes); full `npm run test:e2e:headless` also green on every Chromium/WebKit project (Firefox blocked by the pre-existing sandbox defect in `TROUBLESHOOTING.md`, confirmed unrelated). No acceptance-scenario re-drive — none of the 9 fixed scenarios touches bisect naming. User decided explicitly (2026-09-17, at `core-021`'s own check-in) to diverge from V1 for least-surprise rather than keep the faithfully-reproduced but axis-inconsistent naming — resolves the open judgment call `TODO/VERIFY.md`'s `core-021` entry logged rather than decided unilaterally. Scoped in `TODO/done/core-022-bisect-negative-always-min-side.md` |
| ✅ `edge-fillet` *(decision, no code)* | Arbitrary-**edge** fillet/chamfer — requires forking to a dual-kernel architecture with `OpenCascade.js` | Operation, 1 | user answer to the OpenCascade.js question | ✅ decided (2026-09-17) — **Option 1: ship rim-only, drop edge fillet from the catalog, permanently.** `core-003` proved arbitrary-edge fillet is unachievable on `@jscad/modeling` at any adapter cost (no stable edge/face identity to select against, not a missing function); rim fillet/chamfer (`ui-027`) is the catalog's whole fillet/chamfer surface now, matching what V1 and the reference designs (`picam-2`/`picam-touch`) already do without edge fillets. No `OpenCascade.js` dependency added, no dual-kernel fork scoped. Not deferred — re-opens only if a real design need overturns this explicitly, the same bar this project's other closed architectural decisions use. Recorded in `docs/decisions/geometry-kernel.md`'s addendum. **This milestone's own last open row — M4 is now fully ✅ and needs the human free-form pass before M5 starts**, per `CLAUDE.md`'s Verification section |

## M11 — Gears: the `jscad-gears` library, ported and extended

[↑ Contents](#contents)

The user's jscad1 projects lean on `@jwc/jscad-gears` (an MIT port of Hessmer's involute
builder) and WarpCad's Library has no gear at all. Reading the four projects that use it
(`window-motor`, `drive-wheel`, `bf-gear`, `guide-mount`) found what they need and what V1
never provided — the evidence table is in `core-039`:

- **Ported**: external spur gears with `circularPitch` / `pressureAngle` (14.5°, 20°, **35°**
  all in use) / `clearance` / `backlash` / `profileShift` / `centerHoleDiameter`; meshed pairs
  (`GearSet`); internal ring gears; racks; the old `twist` option (helical).
- **Missing**, so *new*: a **real bevel gear** (`drive-wheel` fakes one with an `enlarge()`
  loft and hand-tuned `snap` offsets), **bevel-pair placement**, a **worm and worm wheel**
  (`drive-mount`'s worm is a cylinder placeholder), **herringbone**, **planetary sets**, and
  **hub/bore/D-flat/keyway/set-screw** options every V1 project bolted on by hand.
- **Defects not to carry over**: `GearSet.gearRatio` is `n/n` (always 1), the rack is
  hard-coded to 41 teeth, a ring gear cannot be built without its pinion, gear type is
  encoded in the sign of `toothCount`.

**The shape**: one framework-free module family in `src/core/gear/` (profile maths, mesh maths,
bevel, worm, planetary) behind one native op (`gear.profile`) and a handful of **composite
recipes**, so helical, herringbone and bevel reuse `profile.extrudeLinear` (twist) and the
existing loft rather than adding native ops. Meshing is a *pure function of two gears'
params* (`core-044`), used by both a ready-made pair and the interactive **Mesh** operation
(`ui-058`) — creation (drag a gear into the view) and operation (select exactly two gears,
Mesh) stay separate lists, per `CLAUDE.md`. Every meshing claim is proven by a
boolean-intersect volume ≈ 0 test with a wrong-phase negative control, not by looking at it.

| id | Task | Kind | Depends | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `core-039` | **Decision:** gear generation (analytic involute vs. porting the cutter sweep), parameter vocabulary (`module` vs `circularPitch`), V1 defects not to port, licence of a port | Decision, M | — | — (reviewed by the user before `core-040`) |
| `core-040` | Involute spur profile generator `src/core/gear/` + V1 parity fixtures from `jscad-gears` | Creation | `core-039` | unit only |
| `core-041` | `gear.profile` native op and `gear.spur` composite (bore, named parts, V1 alias table) | Creation | `core-040` | 1, 3, 10; `core-020/024` repros |
| `core-042` | Helical and herringbone (helix angle → extrude twist, hand, normal vs transverse module) | Creation | `core-041` | 1, 3, 10 |
| `core-043` | Internal (ring) gear without a `connectedGear`, and a true-length rack | Creation | `core-041` | 1, 3, 10 |
| `core-044` | Mesh maths (centre distance, phase, signed ratio, compatibility) and `gear.pair` | Creation | `core-041` | 1, 3, 10 |
| `core-045` | Straight bevel gear — pitch cone, Tredgold teeth, replaces V1's faked bevel | Creation | `core-041` | 1, 3, 10 |
| `core-046` | Bevel-pair and crossed-axis placement; the `drive-wheel` four-gear train without fudge offsets | Creation | `core-044`, `core-045` | 1, 3, 10 |
| `core-047` | Worm and worm wheel (reuses `extrudeHelical`), self-locking readout | Creation | `core-042`, `core-044` | 1, 3, 9, 10 |
| `core-048` | Hub, bore, D-flat, keyway, nut trap, set screw, lightening holes — named parts | Creation | `core-041` | 3, 6 |
| `core-049` | Planetary set (`R = S + 2P`, equal-spacing constraint, ratios, carrier) | Creation | `core-043`, `core-044` | 1, 3, 10 |
| `ui-057` | **Gears** Library section, drag-to-place, parametric Inspector with derived readouts, `docs/gears.md` | Creation | `core-041`, `core-048`, `ui-052` | 1, 3, 6, 7, 10; `ui-053` repro |
| `ui-058` | **Mesh** operation — exactly two gears selected, centre distance + phase, compatibility messages | Operation | `ui-057`, `core-044` | 1, 3, 6, 7, 10, 16, 19; `ui-035/036/053` repros |
| `testing-002` | Acceptance scenario **19** (place two gears, mesh, union, save, export), driven live once | Testing | `ui-057`, `ui-058` | creates 19 |
| `docs-021` | Gear media and a "porting a jscad-gears script" page | Docs | `ui-057`, `ui-058`, `docs-006/007`, `testing-002` | — |

**Suggested order** (the user can hand-use something after each step): `core-039` →
`core-040` → `core-041` → `core-044` → `ui-057` → `ui-058` (**two meshed spur gears, the
`window-motor` and `bf-gear` cases, are now reachable — stop and do the five-minute
free-form pass here, and run scenario 19's steps 1-4 by hand**) → `core-048` → `core-043` →

## M6 — Persistence, export, and timeline history editing

[↑ Contents](#contents)

Added 2026-09-17, directly from the user reading this file and flagging the gap rather
than from a hands-on drive: `CLAUDE.md`'s own Project Structure section has named
`src/storage/` and `src/components/timeline/` ("the feature history list, rollback,
suppress, reorder") since before M0, and its Architecture section has required
transaction-origin-scoped undo/redo the whole time — `ui-012`'s own retirement note
(M1) explicitly built the `supersedes` lineage index "for the undo/redo a later task
needs," and that later task was never named. No milestone through M5 ever scheduled
saving, loading, exporting, or editing a past step; M1-M5 built the document engine and
every op these capabilities need, but never the capabilities themselves. Three new
`TODO/ACCEPTANCE.md` scenarios (10-12) were written alongside this milestone per that
file's own new-capability-class rule, modeled on the concrete case that prompted this:
chamfer an edge 5mm, place a few more items, then decide the chamfer should have been
3mm. Sequenced after M5 for standing-cadence reasons (one human free-form pass between
milestones, per `CLAUDE.md`'s Verification section), not because either M5 row is a
real technical prerequisite for this one.

**Kickoff-level scope, confirmed with the user before this section was written** (the
same status M0's `docs-001`/`docs-002` had for kernel/sync):

- **Persistence targets both local storage and a hosted git backend for v1** — not
  local-only-with-hosted-deferred. Local means IndexedDB autosave of the action log
  plus File System Access Save-As/Open for a portable project file; hosted means the
  "hosted git-based backend" `CLAUDE.md`'s Storage bullet already named, read/written
  directly from the browser with a user-supplied token — the same BYOK pattern the AI
  chat client already uses for its own API key, not a new auth mechanism.
- **Export targets STL, OBJ, and glTF for v1.** STEP (a true parametric interchange
  format, not a mesh) is explicitly out of scope — it would need the same
  `OpenCascade.js` fork question `core-003`/`edge-fillet` already declined for
  arbitrary-edge fillet, and revisiting that is its own decision, not a rider on this
  milestone.

Per `CLAUDE.md`'s Interaction Model section, no UI-surface row below gets scaffolded
until its own precondition/trigger/feedback answers exist — `data-002` is this
milestone's `ui-001`, and every row after it depends on it directly or transitively.
Grouped by mechanism, not by literal control, per this file's own granularity decision
above: one data-layer task for mid-history edit+replay (used by both the timeline panel
and, implicitly, by every future op), one task per storage backend (local vs. hosted
differ enough in real ways — auth, network failure, conflict on reconnect — to earn
separate rows the way M2's hardware-vs-kit-part split did), and one UI task per distinct
surface (timeline panel, save/load, export) rather than three controls crammed into one
task's Testing section.

| id | Task | Kind | Depends | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ `data-001` | Persistence/export Library decision — IndexedDB wrapper choice, File System Access usage, a GitHub API client for the hosted backend, and STL/OBJ/glTF serialization (`@jscad/io`, which pairs with the committed kernel but isn't installed yet, vs. three.js's own exporters, which run off the rendered mesh instead) — each compared with a real rejection table per `CLAUDE.md`'s Library decisions process, written to `docs/decisions/persistence-export.md` | — (architecture) | `docs-002` | none directly — scoped in `TODO/data-001-persistence-export-library-decision.md` |

## M7 — Color, mirror, the Screw hardware family, and a Bill-of-Materials panel

[↑ Contents](#contents)

Scoped 2026-09-17 from a real-usage survey of `~/src/jscad/`, done at the user's own
request to check that every earlier milestone's scope — "colors, holes, groups" named
explicitly — actually covers what the user's own corpus of real parts uses. The survey
excluded vendored/bundled library copies (`dist/`, `node_modules`, compiled webpack
chunks, `csg.js`/`openscad.js` vendor copies) and covered ~644 authored files across
~80 real project directories plus the `jscad-utils`/`jscad-hardware`/`jscad-raspberrypi`
source this project already ports from. Full method and counts are the survey agent's
own report from this session; the findings below are the real, previously-unflagged
gaps it found, one correction to that same survey's own first pass (mirror — see
below), and two already-decided-against non-gaps worth recording so they aren't
re-derived later.

**Groups and holes — already covered, no new rows needed.** Groups are used in ~51 of
~80 projects and are already this project's best-covered mechanism (`ui-015`, `ui-016`,
`group.assemble`/`.snap`/`.align`, every `hardware.*`/`kit.*`/`raspberrypi.*` composite).
Holes are universally built by cylinder-subtraction plus the `Hardware.Bolt`/`Nut`/
`Washer`/`Bearing` recipes this project has already ported (`core-010`, `core-011`,
`core-023`) — the one real remaining gap for holes, a boolean operand addressing a
composite's hidden sub-part (e.g. subtracting a bolt's own `tap` to cut its clearance
hole), is **already named and reserved as `core-024`** in M5 above; this milestone does
not duplicate it.

**Color is the real gap, and it's a regression, not just a missing feature.** `.color(
name)` / `.color(name, alpha)` is used in ~65 of ~80 surveyed projects — more
consistently than fillet/chamfer or snap/align, both of which this project already
treats as core surface. Grepping `src/core/kernel/` and `src/render/mesh.js` directly
confirms there is **no color field anywhere in the document model, the op registry, or
the Inspector** — `createBodyMaterial()` exists in `src/render/mesh.js` but is called
with one of four fixed theme-palette colors (`body`/`selected`/`secondary`/`ghost`),
never a per-body value. Worse: this project's own already-shipped recipes **silently
dropped** color when porting V1 source that used it — `hardware.washer.js`'s and
`hardware.nut.js`'s module docs discuss at length why `clearance`'s V1 `.color('red')`
part became `hidden: true` here, but neither mentions that V1's `washer`/`nut` parts
were *also* explicitly colored (`.color('gray')`) and that information was dropped
without comment. `jscad-raspberrypi` goes further still and uses the alpha channel
(`Parts.Board(...).color('green', 0.75)`), which this project has no representation for
at all.

**Correction (2026-09-17, same day): the first pass of this survey was wrong about
mirror, and the user caught it from direct knowledge of their own projects before this
file was corrected.** The original grep pass reported "0 real hits after excluding
vendored bundles," which was a scoping bug, not a true negative — it searched a
narrower file set than the rest of this survey and missed real project subdirectories
entirely. Re-run directly, scoped the same way as every other count in this milestone
(real project dirs only, vendored `csg.js`/`OpenJSCAD.org`/`docs`/`min.js` copies
excluded): `.mirror(`/`.mirrored(`/`.mirrorX(`/`.mirrorY(`/`.mirrorZ(` appear in **at
least 15 real project files**, not zero — `scope-ring/index.js:52`
(`cableTie.mirrorY()`), `solar-panel-bracket`, `pvc-joints`, `cube-hook`,
`window-motor` (four files), `gate-parts`, `meshboard-bracket`, `light-mount`,
`drive-mount`, `guide-mount` (two files), `scope-jack2` (three files), `cord-wrap` and
`cord-wrap-orig` — plus, notably, **`jscad-raspberrypi/src/spacer.js` and
`boardutils.js` themselves**, the library `core-018` already ported `spacer` from.
`core-018`'s own retirement note recorded that one specific mirror call
(`mirroredY()` on a rounded rectangle) needed no mirror op because reflecting a
rounded rectangle about `y = 0` is the same shape turned by the negated angle — a
trick that happens to work for that one symmetric profile, not evidence the general
operation is unneeded. `mirror` is added back below as a real task, not folded into
the "won't build" note.

**Hull and a dedicated pattern/array helper, re-checked with the same corrected
method, hold up as genuinely unused — not folded into the same mistake.** Every
non-library `.hull(`/`\bhull(` hit outside `scope-ring/index.js` traces to
`OpenJSCAD.org`'s own stock example files, and that one hit is two lines, both
commented out (`// const { hull } = require('@jscad/modeling').hulls;` and
`//   .hull([clampCyl.translateX(4)])`, `scope-ring/index.js:6,61`) — dead code, not a
missed real usage the way mirror was. The apparent `.pattern(`/`.array(` hits (16 of
them) are a different false trail, not a scoping bug: every one resolves to
`jscad-utils`' `Group.prototype.array(parts, map)` (`jscad-utils/src/group.js:529`),
which converts a *named group's parts* into a plain JS array for iteration — unrelated
to placing repeated copies of a shape in a grid or pattern. No project in this corpus
does that with a dedicated helper; repeated placement is still always a hand-rolled
loop. Both are recorded as **deliberately not being added**, on this now-doubly-checked
evidence. Angled cuts, the other candidate gap, are already fully covered by
`transform.rotate` + `boolean.subtract`, which ~61 of 80 projects already use for
exactly that, against only ~7 using the dedicated `.wedge()` shape. Text/label is real
but rare and always indirect (~12 projects, via `jscad-utils`' own `label()` wrapper
around `vector_text()`) — named below as a single low-priority reserved row rather than
either a full task or a silent gap.

## M8 — Sketch primitives: a sketch is a way of defining a solid

[↑ Contents](#contents)

`TODO/sketch-001` replaced ui-020's free-standing Sketch mode (loose `sketch.line`
records the kernel never saw) with a **Sketch primitive**: a Library row whose drop opens
an editor, outlines marked Solid or Hole on one or more slices, and one extrusion method
(Linear, Revolve, Helical, Outline, Loft). It is a body like any other — selectable,
movable, a boolean operand, exportable, persisted, re-editable. Its spec was L/XL and said
to split after steps 1-3; the remaining three steps are the rows below it, with their
spec text moved verbatim. `docs/decisions/interaction-model-qa.md` section 7 was rewritten
for it and is **awaiting the user's confirmation** (every default marked UNCONFIRMED, and
listed in `TODO/VERIFY.md`).

## M9 — AI partner: a model that drives the same document the person does

[↑ Contents](#contents)

`CLAUDE.md`'s AI Partner section has been a promise since kickoff and nothing under
`src/ai/` or `mcp/` exists. The milestone is built backwards from two prompts the user
wrote, kept verbatim as scenarios 17 and 18 (`ai-010`):

> Use the sketch tool to create an airfoil for a model plane wing, create the wing then the
> rest of the model so I can 3D print it.
>
> Here is a drawing of a raspberry pi 7 create a group object to be added to the library.

**The shape**: the AI is a second writer on the existing NDJSON action log, through the
same command layer, with origin `ai` (in-browser chat) or `mcp` (the server). One
framework-free tool registry (`ai-002`) serves both. It is derived from the kernel
registry so creation and operation stay separate tools with operand counts, the same rule
`CLAUDE.md` sets for the UI. Several rows beyond the AI plumbing are needed because neither prompt is reachable
with today's capabilities:

- **Airfoil + wing** needs curve helpers and per-slice offset/twist (`sketch-008`), a way
  for the model to *see and measure* its result (`ai-007`) and print prep (`io-007`).
- **Drawing → library group** needs the reference image (`sketch-003`, already scoped),
  user-defined library parts (`core-037`, `ui-055`; the Library is a fixed recipe list
  today) and the image/overlay tools (`ai-008`).

Per `CLAUDE.md`, a UI-surface row here is not done until reachable from `/`, documented,
and re-driven against the acceptance scenarios named in its file. The assistant's own
checks run against a scripted provider in CI and against a **real key by hand** for 17/18:
a scripted transcript proves plumbing, never that the model's choices are good
(`ai-010`, `ai-011`).

## M10 — Docs media: screenshots and movies generated by Playwright

[↑ Contents](#contents)

`README.md` and `docs/` have no pictures. This milestone makes every picture a by-product of a
test, so a UI change that makes a documented image wrong fails a check instead of rotting.

**The shape**: `*.docs.js` specs under `e2e/docs/` drive the app at `/`; stills use
`toHaveScreenshot` with the baseline directory set to `docs/screenshots/`, so
`--update-snapshots` rewrites the documentation image; movies are recorded as video and
converted to GIF under `docs/media/` with ffmpeg. A guard in the `guards` project fails on
dead links, orphaned images and stale movies. Movies are scripted recordings and do **not**
replace driving `TODO/ACCEPTANCE.md` by hand.

## M12 — Project variables: one named value drives any parameter of any part

[↑ Contents](#contents)

The jscad1 projects open with a top-level `const piVersion = 4` and every part reads it; to
move a case from a Pi 4 to a Pi 5 you change one line. WarpCad can only do that by finding the
Pi body and editing its `model` param, then repairing whatever assumed the old one. The only
cross-record link today is `condition.js`'s `when: { ref, param, in }`, which *reads* one
body's param and cannot *drive* anything.

**The shape**: variables are **log records** (`var.define`), so undo, the transaction origin,
NDJSON, gist sync and the AI all work with no new mechanism. A param is bound with plain JSON,
`{ "$var": "piModel" }`, in its own slot (or one element of a vector param), and a single
resolution point in the fold substitutes it — so **no op knows variables exist and every op
gets them**, including composites and `core-037` user parts. Type compatibility and limits come
from the registry's own param schemas. `when` gains a `{ var, in }` form so one switch can also
turn cut-outs on and off. **Deliberately not in this milestone**: derived variables and
arithmetic (`outer = inner + 2 * wall`) — the likely next request, but it needs a safe
evaluator and cycle detection, and the `$var` format does not change if it is added later.

| id | Task | Kind | Depends | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ `core-050` | Project variables: `var.define`, `$var` binding in any param, single resolution point, `when: { var }`, impact check over all consumers, rename/delete rewrites, hostile-input caps | Creation, L | `data-004`, `ui-052` | 1, 2, 3, 4, 10, 12, 14, 15; `data-007`, `ui-052`, `ui-044` repros |
| ✅ `ai-014` | AI tools for project variables: `list_variables`, `define_variable`, `set_variable`, `bind_param`, `unbind_param`, `rename_variable`, `delete_variable` in `src/ai/tools/`, schemas, limits, intent, `docs/mcp.md` | Creation, M | `core-050`, `ai-002` | unit only |
| ✅ `ui-059` | **Variables** panel, a bind control on every bindable Inspector field ("New variable from this value…"), chips for bound fields, variable choice in `ConditionRow`, `docs/variables.md` | Creation | `core-050` | 1, 2, 7, 10, 12, 14, 15 + new; `ui-043/044/046/049/052` repros |

**Suggested order**: `core-050` → `ui-059` (the Pi 4 → Pi 5 switch is then reachable by hand —
stop and do the five-minute free-form pass here). Variable tools for the AI are `ai-014` (they
were appended to `ai-002`'s list by `core-050` but not wired; it depends only on `core-050` and
`ai-002`, so it can run any time after `core-050`).

## M15 — A mergeable project file: the AI as a second writer

[↑ Contents](#contents)

Every earlier milestone kept the log single-writer by construction: the human is paused while
an AI turn runs, the MCP server edits a closed file and the app asks Reload / Overwrite /
Cancel, and a stale hosted save asks Overwrite / Load / Cancel. Each of those answers throws one
side's work away. On 2026-10-12 the user reversed the "no CRDT" decision: the AI is a second
user of the same file, and its edits should merge.

**The shape**: a hand-rolled, **state-based** merge, with **no CRDT library** and **no change of
storage format**. The file stays ordered NDJSON record lines plus one trailing state line; the
merge metadata (a position key per row, last-writer-wins stamps, tombstones, a project id) goes
in that state line, so record lines stay byte-identical. Record ids become replica-scoped. A
merged row that no longer builds is **flagged broken, never re-parented**, through `ui-052`'s
existing Leave-broken mechanism. **Deliberately not in this milestone**: live multi-person
editing over a network (there is still no server), tombstone compaction, importing a *different*
project's bodies, and any change to `ai-012`'s relay.

The user's eight answers (conflict policy, shared rollback marker, version 2, whole-record
edits, live viewport during a turn with a Watch toggle, automatic file merge with Undo merge,
chip-plus-banner conflicts, all four storage paths) are recorded in `docs-022`.

| id | Task | Kind | Depends | Acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ✅ `docs-022` | Decision record: `document-sync.md` to **Committed**, options and rejection tables re-checked, the eight answers, `CLAUDE.md` Stack row and AI Partner section; **user accepts before code** | Decision, M | none | none |
| ✅ `data-008` | Replica id and Lamport clock per document instance; `nextId` becomes replica-scoped; legacy ids untouched | Core, M | `docs-022` | 10, 12, 14, 21; `data-004`, `ai-002`, `core-056` repros |
| `data-009` | `src/core/merge/`: position keys, last-writer-wins registers, tombstones, `mergeStates`, relatedness, diff; three-replica property suite | Core, L | `data-008` | none (nothing imports it yet) |
| `data-010` | `document.js` stamps every mutation, removal is a tombstone, the rollback marker is an anchor, `mergeFrom` re-folds and flags conflicts | Core, L | `data-009` | 1, 4, 10, 12, 14, 15, 21; `data-003/004/007`, `core-030/056`, `ui-052`, `ai-006` repros |
| `data-011` | Project file version 2: merge section in the state line, hostile-input caps, legacy files byte-identical, newer-version refusal | Core, M | `data-010` | 8, 10, 14, 21; `io-001`, `ai-003`, `ai-009`, `core-037` repros |
| ✅ `ui-062` | **File ▾ → Merge file…**, merge toast and banner, conflict chip reason, **Undo merge**, `docs/merging.md` | Operation, L | `data-011` | 7, 10, 12, 14 + new **22**; `ui-033/043/052`, `ai-006` repros |
| ✅ `ai-017` | Keep editing while the Assistant works: the turn lands by merge, header chip, **Watch** toggle; decision 5 amended | Control, L | `ui-062` | 1, 4, 7, 12, 17, 18, 21, 22 + new **24**; `ai-002/006/014`, `core-056`, `ui-032/043` repros |
| `data-012` | *(reserved)* Tombstone and position-key compaction, once `data-011` has measured real file growth | Core | `data-011` | 10 |
| `io-012` | *(reserved)* Import another project's bodies into this one (id remap), the case `ui-062` refuses as "a different project" | Creation | `ui-062` | new |
| `ai-019` | *(reserved)* Standalone live-bridge host: `mcp/server.js --live` with a loopback WebSocket (`ws` 8.x, health check re-run) for a built or hosted app, Chromium/Firefox only (WebKit measured blocked) | Control | `ai-012` | live-bridge scenario |
| `ai-018` | *(reserved)* Relax `ai-012`'s one-writer refusals ("the user is editing; try again") now that a merge exists | Control | `ai-012`, `ai-017` | live-bridge scenario |

**Suggested order**: `docs-022` → `data-008` → `data-009` → `data-010` → `data-011` → `ui-062`
(a merge is then reachable by hand on every engine — do a free-form pass here, with a real
second device) → `ai-017` (the visible payoff) → `io-010` → `io-011`. The last three depend only
on `ui-062` and can run in any order.

**Cross-engine coverage is inside the milestone**, per `CLAUDE.md` Browser Support: `data-011`,
`ui-062`, `io-011` and `ai-017` each run all three engines; `io-010`'s file-handle path is
Chromium-only by platform and its spec covers Firefox and WebKit through Merge file….

**Milestone end**: the five-minute free-form human pass, at phone, tablet and laptop, with two
real devices on one hosted project and a real Assistant turn running while editing.

in one place. **Historical; do not update it.** Where it disagrees with the table above, the table above
is right — e.g. it says M7 has "9 open" rows and M6 "holds `core-029`", both since closed.

|---|---|
