| M1 — Viewport scaffold + first primitive creation | ✅ **Done** (7/7 tasks) |
| M1.5 — Selection/gizmo fixes from hands-on use | ✅ **Done** (7/7 tasks) |
| M2 — Hardware + kit-part catalog, snap-guide placement | ✅ **Done** (4/4 tasks) |
| M3 — Booleans, selection-count gating, group/sub-part selection | ✅ **Done** (7/7 tasks) |
| M4+ — Raspberry Pi accessory catalog, sketch mode, flagged-risk follow-ups | ✅ **Done** (13/13 tasks) |
| M5 — Hardware Library surface, closing the fastener reachability gap | ✅ **Done** — `ui-030` ✅ done (2026-09-17), `ui-031` ✅ done (2026-09-18), `core-024` ✅ done (2026-09-18), `ui-035` ✅ done (2026-09-18), `ui-036` ✅ done (2026-09-18) — the whole composite catalogue is now reachable, `ACCEPTANCE.md` scenarios 3 and 6 no longer need their primitive stand-ins, and a composite now keeps its named parts through a transform so a *placed* part can be drilled into and addressed by a boolean. **Scenario 5's two kernel blockers are closed, and `ui-035` has now closed the first of its two UI ones**: the Snap/Align popover emits a sub-part operand by hand, proven live against a real Pi 5. `ui-040` (the same route for the *boolean* buttons, filed by `core-024`) is still open and still blocked behind `ui-032`, which is also scenario 5's remaining blocker in its own right — an enclosed body cannot be picked at all. `ui-035` also named a third, narrower picking gap for `ui-032`'s scope to weigh: an *occluded* sub-part (a mounting hole passing through a PCB) cannot be drilled into either, since the raycast stops at the part in front of it. `ui-036` (angled Bisect controls) is done: a tilted cut is now reachable from the Bisect popover, and its six-field popover had to be lifted above the phone Inspector sheet to stay clickable at 390px. `core-025` ✅ done (2026-09-18) — the probe-and-bake helper set is now one module, `rpi-recipe-kit.js`, with four private ~200-line copies deleted (`recipes/` down 1,047 lines) and every recipe's output byte-identical. `ui-040` ✅ done (2026-09-20) — **the last open row of M5, so this milestone is now complete.** The boolean buttons emit `{ id, pieces }` through the same `selectedOperands` computed `ui-035` built, so the two share one code path rather than two; `selection.js` was not touched, because the state the file said was unrepresentable already was. **Scenario 5 passes hand-driven for the first time**, measured: a Pi drilled to `ethernet` cuts 1 207.743 mm³ out of a 40 mm cube where the whole board cuts 5 661.317 mm³, while a bolt's `tap` and the whole bolt are identical to the digit (986.379 mm³ each), which is this project's own recorded correction confirmed from the UI. Two things stay open and are named rather than absorbed: a **fully enclosed** body still cannot be selected by any hand route — `ui-032` is **not** the cure, checked rather than assumed, because a History row is a row selection and never a body selection (`data-002` §13) — and `ui-035`'s occluded-sub-part raycast gap is the same limitation one layer down. Both are filed as `ui-041` |
| M6 — Persistence, export, and timeline history editing | 🔶 **In progress** — `data-001` ✅ done (2026-09-18): the milestone's blocking Library decision is resolved in `docs/decisions/persistence-export.md`, so every other row can now name real modules. Picked `idb` 8.0.3 for IndexedDB, **no library** for File System Access (feature-detect + download/upload fallback), **hand-rolled `fetch`** over Octokit for the git backend (43.8 KB gzip measured = 16.7% of the app bundle, for three endpoints), and a **split** export path. Three findings change downstream rows rather than just recording preferences: **`@jscad/io` ships no glTF serializer at all** (404), so STL/OBJ come from the kernel (`src/core/export.js`) and glTF only from three.js (`src/render/export.js`) — **`io-003`'s Affected files needs updating**; three.js's `STLExporter` fed by this repo's own mesh bridge emits a **non-watertight** solid (100 of 362 edges unmatched), which is why STL goes kernel-side; and File System Access is **desktop-Chromium-only (~31%, no Android)**, so the fallback is the *primary* path and `ui-033` must not label a control "Save" when it can only download a copy. `data-002` ✅ done (2026-09-19): the Interaction-model Q&A for every M6 control is in `docs/decisions/interaction-model-qa.md` sections 8-19 and `ACCEPTANCE.md` scenarios 10-12 are rewritten against real control names. Every global control (Undo, Redo, History, File ▾ with Save/Open/Export/Hosted) lives in the **header bar**, not the Operations toolbar or nav chrome; the user changed two of the agent's drafted answers — hosted saves gain an **opt-in Auto-push** (off by default; a 409 pauses it and raises the conflict dialog), and **rollback is Fusion-style** (creation stays enabled and new steps insert at the marker), which puts an insert-at-index primitive and an inactive-tail shadow check on `data-004`. `ui-033`, `ui-034`, `io-002` and `io-003` carry "Superseded by `data-002`" notes. `data-003` ✅ done (2026-09-19): Undo/Redo are live in the header bar, the first two occupants of `data-002`'s "document bar" cluster, with `AppView.vue` now carrying a right-aligned slot sized for History and File ▾ to land beside them. The open design question resolved **against** inverse-append: undo **removes the step and re-folds the log** through the same `ingest` path an append uses, because section 18's confirmed answer is "only Undo removes a recent step" and a `consumes` record has no honest inverse. That makes redo literally an **insert-at-index**, so the primitive section 18 puts on `data-004` is already built, exercised and atomic on failure. Only `origin: 'local'` steps enter the stack; undo refuses **by name** rather than orphaning a record something else still references. `data-004` ✅ done (2026-09-19): the whole timeline **data layer** is built — edit a past step's params in place with full downstream replay, suppress/unsuppress, a static `canMove` plus reorder, and Fusion-style rollback with insert-at-the-marker and the inactive-tail shadow check — all as **one widened primitive**, `rebuildFrom(nextEntries, nextRollbackAt)`, not a second replay engine beside `data-003`'s. The contract `ui-032` was blocked on is now typed rather than prose: `ReplayRejection` carries a `BrokenStep` (`{ id, step, label, reason }`) for an atomic rejection on the active document, `brokenSteps()`/`TimelineRow.broken` carry the same shape informationally for the inactive tail, and there are exactly two reason shapes (`needs <body name>`, `can't be built: <why>`). `appendAll` inserts at the marker on its own, so drag-place, the toolbar, the gizmo, the Inspector and Sketch all obey section 18's "nothing is blocked" without knowing the marker exists. Full re-fold **measured** rather than assumed (67 ms for 200 fillet-heavy records, linear, kernel-dominated) so the incremental version stays unbuilt. `ui-029`'s freeze was revisited as asked and turned out to be a *different question*, not a wrong answer — but it surfaced a real stated gap: a `transform.rotate` row is **not editable at all** today, because `core-004` carries rotation absolutely. Section 18's "Unsure" was measured (36% of roll-back-then-move cases break a tail row) and **filed as `data-006` rather than built**. No UI and no e2e, by design: scenario 12 stays a partial pass until `ui-032` gives it a trigger. `io-001` ✅ done (2026-09-19): **autosave, silent restore and the `save-status` chip are live** — reload and the document reopens exactly as left (same bodies, same millimetre positions, suppressed rows and the rollback marker included, undo empty by design, camera re-framed on the content), a blocked/private/full IndexedDB degrades to a persistent red chip with the app fully usable, and the file Save/Open **mechanism** exists with a first-class `<a download>`/`<input type=file>` fallback (the primary path on every phone, tablet, Firefox and Safari) plus a capability check `ui-033` labels its menu from. The task's key finding came from *driving* the app, not reading it: the specified flush on `pagehide` does **not** survive a reload in real Chromium — an IndexedDB write started while the page is dying is never committed — so a synchronous `localStorage` safety copy backs it (a solid placed then reloaded inside 500 ms was being lost). One on-disk format for autosave and file (`src/core/project.js`; a `project-state` line only when there is state to carry). Multi-tab is last-writer-wins by decision. **No File ▾ menu, Ctrl+S/O, hosted or export yet** — scenario 10's file and hosted halves stay undriven until `ui-033`/`io-002`. `io-002` ✅ done (2026-09-19): the **hosted half's whole mechanism** is built and driven — a hand-rolled `fetch` client against three GitHub REST endpoints (plus the blob endpoint, because the Contents API silently returns *empty content* above 1 MB and reading that as an empty project would commit a truncated log over a real one), the first real **credential boundary** in this codebase (`src/storage/tokenStorage.js`, written generically so the eventual BYOK chat client reuses it rather than inventing a second one), the **auto-push scheduler** as pure fake-timer-testable logic, and a Pinia store exposing exactly the API `ui-033` calls. The conflict answer `document-sync.md` named as its revival condition is now concrete: the Contents API's `sha` **is** the mechanism, a stale one is refused rather than forced, and the three resolutions are Overwrite / Load / Cancel with **no merge, ever** — plus two rules §11 implies that the task file never stated, namely that the *first* Save into a repository that already holds a project file this session never loaded also goes down the conflict path, and that Cancel leaves the dot at `!` because dismissing a dialog is not resolving a divergence. The credential rules are enforced, not merely intended: the API origin is allowlisted at client construction, the token goes in an `Authorization` header and nowhere else, **no server error text is ever echoed into a message** (GitHub's own body can quote the credential it rejected), and the token is not in Pinia state, not in a getter, and never shown again after entry. **Still no UI** — the File ▾ hosted group, Connect dialog, conflict modal and status dot are `ui-033`'s, and scenario 10's hosted half stays NOT DRIVEABLE until a human has a scratch repo and a token. `io-003` ✅ done (2026-09-19): the **export serialization mechanism** is built and verified byte-for-byte, with **no UI** — `src/core/export.js` writes STL and OBJ from the kernel's `Geom3` (via the two `@jscad` serializers `data-001` chose) and `src/render/export.js` writes a binary `.glb` through a lazy-loaded three.js `GLTFExporter`; neither downloads anything, that is `ui-034`. The split held up exactly as `data-001` predicted: a real evaluated union re-read by numpy and three.js's own loaders (nothing shared with the serializer) came back at 286 triangles, the kernel's volume to 1.3e-5 relative, and **0 of 429 edges unmatched**, and the cube-minus-Ø6-bore shape where three.js's `STLExporter` left 100 of 362 edges open came out at 0 of 438. Two decisions the file left open are made: **several bodies are one combined file** (Group-valued bodies export as their `combine()`d solid), and glTF is **binary `.glb`**. One the file did not anticipate: glTF is metres and Y-up, so a root node carries the conversion from the document's mm / Z-up rather than shipping a model a thousand times too large and lying down. Refusals are typed and named (`nothing-to-export`, `empty-geometry`, `evaluation-failed`, `stale-selection`) and `ui-034` gets a pure scope resolver and filename helper. The main bundle is byte-identical until `ui-034` imports it. Scenario 11 stays NOT DRIVEABLE (recorded, not faked) until `ui-034` gives it a trigger. `ui-032` ✅ done (2026-09-20): the **History panel** is live — list every step, select one to preview the document as it stood (read-only, amber-outlined, with Undo/Redo/Operations/Library disabled while previewing), edit a step's params in place with full downstream replay, suppress, reorder (up/down buttons), and Fusion-style rollback, all as thin wiring over `data-004`'s single `rebuildFrom` primitive; **not split**. Scenario 12 now PASSES in full for the first time (laptop/Chromium + phone/WebKit) — one honest caveat: its step 12's literal setup (a fillet over an existing chamfer on the same face) is refused by `core-003`'s non-prismatic-rim guard, so the broken-replay path was driven in an equivalent shape and the scenario's wording, not the guard, is what's off. The `ui-016` occluded-sub-part picking gap was weighed and kept **out** of scope (a list needs no raycast); it belongs with `ui-040`. `ui-040` and `data-006` are now unblocked. `ui-033` ✅ done (2026-09-21): the **File ▾ menu** is live in the header bar — **Save/Save As…** where the browser can write a file in place and **Download copy** everywhere else (never a "Save" that only downloads), **Open…** behind a replace-confirm that appears *before* the picker so a cancelled picker can never lose work, **Ctrl/Cmd+S** that provably makes zero network requests, and a **Hosted storage** group (Connect repository… dialog with an uncontrolled, never-shown-back token field; Save to / Load from; opt-in **Auto-push** with the shape-coded File-button dot; the three-button conflict dialog that never overwrites and is deferred while a drag is in progress). `hosted.start()` is wired at boot *after* the local restore, which §11 rule 3's "is the restored document ahead of the last push?" needs. **Scenario 10's hosted half was driven against a mocked `api.github.com` only — against a REAL scratch repo it is still NOT DRIVEABLE here and is not claimed.** The native File System Access picker is an OS dialog and is proven against a fake only (`TODO/VERIFY.md`). Two real defects were found by driving, not by the suite, and fixed: after Cancel the auto-push item promised "will retry", and a stale refused-file error hid every later hosted success (errors are now dismissible and the newer notice wins). `ui-034` ✅ done (2026-09-21): **File ▾ → Export…** is live — the last item of the File menu's "this device" group, opening a popover with a **Scope** choice (defaults to the selection, else the whole scene; the Selection radio is disabled at 0 selected; several bodies are one file and the line says so) and a **Format** choice (STL default, OBJ, glTF `.glb`, remembered per viewer), a summary line naming the exact file, `Exported union-1.stl` on the chip, and any refusal shown **by name** in the popover, which stays open. It is **dimmed with its reason printed under it** (`Nothing to export`; `Return to the latest step to export` while History previews) and stays enabled while rolled back, labelled `(rolled back — N later steps not included)`. The document changing under an open popover is answered by deriving every line live: an *explicitly* chosen Selection that vanishes blocks Export rather than widening to the scene. One shared download path (`storage/download.js`) now serves both Download copy and Export. **Scenario 11 is driven for the first time and PASSES for everything this task owns (laptop/tablet/phone, Chromium and WebKit), but the bytes are not clean: the STL/OBJ of a plain Union and of a placed bolt have 9 and ~40 unmatched edges (T-junctions, volume correct) where `io-003` measured 0 on the two shapes it checked — a pre-existing serializer defect, filed as `io-004` (`priority: high`), not fixed here — and no real slicer or glTF viewer has opened any file.** `io-004` ✅ done (2026-09-21): **every STL and OBJ WarpCad writes is now a closed manifold**, which `io-003` believed and had only measured on two shapes. The cause was inside the serializer's own repair rather than missing from it: `modifiers.generalize` **snaps to a bounding-box-derived grid (4.8e-4 mm for the union) and only then** asks `insertTjunctions` whether a vertex is on an edge, against a **fixed 1e-5 mm** — so a snap that displaces a collinear vertex by up to 4.2e-4 mm, some 40× that tolerance, makes the junction invisible to it. Axis-aligned T-junctions survive the snap exactly, which is why `io-003`'s two shapes read 0 and a solid placed at 66.93 mm or a hex head's diagonal rim did not. Both fixes the task file proposed were **measured and rejected** — `generalize(...)` and `retessellate` change nothing (6 / 40 / 24 before and after), because the serializer already calls that very pass. What shipped is `src/core/kernel/watertight.js`, which inserts the missing vertices itself using the serializer's snap grid **only as an identity key** and never rounding a coordinate, so the exported bounding box is bit-identical and vertex sharing survives the serializer's own later snap. 0 open edges on all eight shapes measured, in both formats and under both readings of the criterion, no zero-area triangles, volume drift unchanged, io-003's own two shapes byte-for-byte as before. Scenario 11 re-driven at laptop/Chromium and phone/WebKit: the union's STL reads 206 triangles, 0 of 309 open (was 9 of 300); a placed bolt 0 of 342 (was 40 of 302). **Two things found and filed rather than folded in**: the `.glb` comes from the render mesh and is still open (44 of 262 on the same union) — `io-005`; and the repair roughly doubles an export that already ran synchronously on the main thread (22.9 → 48.1 ms for 24 bodies) — `perf-001`. **Still nobody has opened an exported file in a real slicer.** `io-005` ✅ done (2026-09-21): **the `.glb` is now closed too, and the prior question is closed with it.** The task's first job was a decision, not a fix — `io-003` argued a glTF consumer is a viewer that does not need a watertight solid — and it is **decided yes and recorded in `docs/decisions/persistence-export.md` sub-decision 4** so nobody re-opens it: the mechanism already existed, an unclosed `.glb` beside a closed STL of the same body is a surprising inconsistency the project had already written a warning about, and the cost is paid once per click. Measured on the bytes of real downloaded files by a parser sharing no code with the exporter: **44→0, 46→0, 271→0, 42→0 and 88→0 open edges** across the five shapes, volume within 4e-8 of the kernel's, outward winding, bounding box bit-identical. The trap the file warned about was real and is the reason this was not a one-liner: the repair inserts vertices *collinear* with the edge they split, and the bridge's corner-0 fan turns those into **zero-area triangles — 101 of 672 on a repaired `raspberrypi.bplus`** — whose normals are undefined; the export path now picks an apex the fan is clean from, falling back to a centroid fan for the few polygons where none is (5 of 217 on that board). Always-centroid was measured and **rejected**: it also reaches 0 but emits 40% more triangles and would double a plain cuboid from 12 to 24. **The viewport is untouched, proven rather than asserted**: `geom3ToBufferGeometry`'s default output is bit-identical float-for-float to its pre-`io-005` output on four shapes, at an unchanged 0.007-0.018 ms per rebuild against the 16 ms budget, so the repair is on the export path and nowhere near the frame loop. Scenarios 11 and 1 re-driven at laptop/Chromium and phone/WebKit with glTF exported *before* STL; `io-004`'s STL/OBJ numbers came back identical (0 of 309, 0 of 342) and a placed bolt's `.glb` went 46→0 of 318. Cost: **+12.1 ms** on a 24-body export and ~15% more bytes, added to `perf-001` rather than filed twice. **A real glTF viewer has still never opened one of these files.** `perf-001` ✅ done (2026-09-21): **export no longer blocks the main thread, and the finding's own premise turned out to be half wrong.** The file assumed `io-004`'s repair was the dominant cost of a slow export; breaking a 24-body export into phases showed **76% of it is `toGeom3()` — the `combine()` of composite bodies, i.e. real kernel booleans — at 43.4 ms against the repair's 28.0 ms and serialization's 8.7 ms**. That single measurement redirected the task: optimising the repair alone moved the *freeze* not at all (224 → 233 ms worst frame gap at 4× CPU throttling), because the freeze was never mostly the repair. **Both separable pieces shipped.** (a) `makeWatertight` is near-linear — integer corner identity instead of a `${ix},${iy},${iz}` string per vertex per pass (the bigger win at reachable sizes), plus the uniform spatial hash the file asked for (which removes a 2.38 M-candidate-per-pass quadratic term on a 16k-polygon solid): **1.71 → 0.39, 27.01 → 4.82, 20.18 → 3.79 and 89.92 → 20.78 ms** across the four benchmark scenes. Its output is **provably unchanged**, which is the bar the file set ("a faster repair that stops finding a T-junction is a regression, not an optimisation"): `bench/watertight-equivalence.mjs` compares the repaired vertex list *in order* and the whole written STL, OBJ and GLB by SHA-256 across all ten shapes `io-004` and `io-005` assert on — **all ten identical**. (b) An **export-only worker** (`src/core/export.worker.js`, the repo's first), because the file's own condition for it was met and measured: after (a), a 24-body export still froze the main thread for **233 ms at 4× CPU and 346 ms at 6×**. The structured-clone cost the decision hinged on was measured rather than assumed — 0.18 ms to pack, 6.5 ms to clone, 0.11 ms for the transferable bytes back, against ~57 ms relocated — so it does not eat the win. Worst frame gap now **27.3 ms at 4× and 39.5 ms at 6×**, with the main thread painting 11-12 frames *during* an export instead of stalling through it. **The general kernel-evaluation RPC was deliberately NOT built**: this worker is entered from the export path alone, no `evaluate()` caller or the document fold changed, and the 43.4 ms `combine()` is filed as `core-029` with the numbers instead. Also shipped: a **pending state** (`Exporting…` plus a `role="status"` line naming the file) after 100 ms — measured to appear **5 of 5** times on a 24-body export and **0 of 5** on a one-body one even at 6× throttling, so a quick export never flashes it; it is only worth having *because* the work moved, since a state set just before a synchronous block cannot paint. Scenario 11 and scenario 1 re-driven at laptop/Chromium and phone/WebKit: every measured byte identical to `io-004`'s and `io-005`'s records (`union-1.stl` 10,384 B / 206 tri / 0 of 309; `union-1.glb` 14,268 B / 184 tri / 0 of 276; a placed bolt 0 of 342 and 0 of 318; a `raspberrypi.bplus` `.glb` 0 of 1023). The production build's worker chunk verified to load and answer in **Chromium and WebKit**. **No real slicer or glTF viewer has still ever opened one of these files.** `ui-041` ✅ done (2026-09-21): **a body no click can reach can now be selected by hand** — an **Objects** list (header toggle between Redo and History, sharing History's dock/sheet slot so no fourth overlay was invented) whose rows select the body they name through the ordinary selection store, with a per-row check as the touch-friendly additive route, so "2 selected" is reachable from the list alone and mixes freely with viewport clicks. **List over pick-cycling** because cycling would put a second meaning on `ui-016`'s second click — checked, not assumed: the drill-down was re-driven and is intact. **The trap the file did not mention was real:** selecting a fully enclosed bolt changed **0 canvas pixels** (the highlight is a material swap and the shell wins the depth test), so "identical to a clicked body" was false on the canvas; fixed with an x-ray twin that draws only the hidden part (~1 186 pixels, Chromium and WebKit). **`ACCEPTANCE.md` scenario 5 is a full pass with a genuinely enclosed operand for the first time:** a bolt entirely inside a 40 mm cube, selected and subtracted from the list alone at laptop, tablet and phone — 61 108.343 mm3, exactly 64 000 - 2 891.657. Its honest limit is filed as `ui-042`: an enclosed body's hidden *sub-parts* still cannot be addressed by a boolean. Two pre-existing defects found by hand-driving: the phone Library sheet stacked behind History's (fixed — the same lines now close Objects too) and the nav chrome overlapping the Operations toolbar at 390px with one body selected (identical before this task; filed as `ui-043`). `ui-042` ✅ done (2026-09-22): **enclosed sub-part addressing now closes scenario 5 for good.** A disclosure chevron (`objects-expand-<id>`) on composite rows only; child rows reuse the ordinary selection store's two gestures. **The operand-order trap the file's own suggested flow walked straight into was real** — tap the part, then add the case, makes the *part's body* the base, backwards from what Subtract needs — fixed by having the additive gesture add a body **at the end** with its depth attached (one depth slot; `selection.count` untouched by it, per `ui-040`'s invariant), so the natural order (case row, then the part's check) reaches the right base, with the toolbar's operand line proving it before commit. `selection.js` gained `selectPart`; `drillInto`'s exactly-one-body rule (`ui-016`) is untouched. `summarizeBody` gained `partNames`/`hiddenPartNames`, reusing `createBodyObject`'s own part-addressability test (extracted as `hasNamedParts`) instead of re-deriving it, so the list and the renderer can never disagree about which body is a composite. Measured: an enclosed Raspberry Pi's `ethernet` cuts **4 545.0 mm³** from a 100x70x40 case versus **23 168.2 mm³** for the whole board (both against the kernel directly, independent of the app). `ui-016` (second click still drills), `ui-040` (viewport sub-part dance), `ui-041` (enclosed whole-body Subtract, 61 108.343 mm3) and `core-020` (a composite does not absorb an earlier body) all re-run clean. `ui-043` ✅ done (2026-09-22): **the phone nav-chrome/Operations-toolbar overlap is closed, with a second overlap the file never measured found and closed alongside it.** The file's own 21px number (one selected, Inspector open) reproduced exactly; investigating further found the "not measured" two-selected + Inspector-open combination overlapped too, by 6px — the 11-icon-button row alone already spent almost the whole 146px budget before the badge's line count was even considered, so unwrapping the badge alone (option (a) as literally stated) would not have closed either case fully. Shipped: option (a) extended — a phone-only short badge string ("select 2+", the full "Select 2 or more solids" untouched in every button's `title`/`aria-label`) plus a small trim to `OperationsToolbar.vue`'s own container gap/padding, confined to its existing `@media (max-width: 640px)` block. **Option (b) (anchor the chrome to the toolbar's real measured bottom) was rejected with a measured reason**, not a preference: the chrome already sits only ~19-20px above the Inspector sheet's own worst-case top, so there was no slack on the chrome side to spend without risking a new overlap against the sheet itself — the toolbar's own footprint was always what had to shrink. Both previously-broken states now measure a uniform 6px clear gap (confirmed by a real drag-and-click hand-drive, not just store-seeded state, in WebKit and Chromium); every previously-safe state (Objects/History sheet open, a composite expanded) kept or grew its margin. `ui-010`, `ui-032`, `ui-035` and `ui-040`'s own original phone-overlap repros all re-run clean. The remaining M6 rows are `data-006` and `core-029` (both held) |

That also explains the arbitrary-looking pattern the finding reported: axis-aligned T-junctions survive a snap exactly, which is every one in `io-003`'s two shapes and none on a hex head's diagonal rim. Ten candidates were measured across eight shapes before any code shipped, and the winner was **not** the obvious one — snapping first and then repairing (generalize's own order) reaches 0 too, but moves coordinates onto the grid, shifted a measured bounding box by 2.3e-4 mm for no benefit and ran ~2× slower; using the snap grid **purely as an identity key** and never rounding a coordinate reaches 0 with a bit-identical bounding box, and is robust for a reason worth writing down: once a vertex is *shared*, the serializer's own later snap cannot undo it, because identical coordinates snap identically. The tolerance is not tuned — a sweep from 0.05ε to 50ε gives byte-identical output. | `ui-053` | 2026-09-28 19:04 | **~22m** (measured from `date -u`: Step 3 preflight — the first `npm run test:unit -- --run` — at 18:42 UTC, committed ~19:04 UTC). Scoped **S-M**; landed as scoped. Root cause, found by a real-mouse Playwright repro against the running dev server with temporary console instrumentation in `placement.js`'s `begin` and `LibraryPanel.vue`'s `onRowPointerDown` (reverted once diagnosed, per the file's own Proposed fix): **no code anywhere handled Escape for the Operations toolbar's three popovers** (Snap/Align, Fillet/Chamfer, Enlarge/Fit/Bisect — one shared `openPopover` ref and one `.ops__popover-backdrop`, teleported to `<body>`, `pointer-events: auto`, `z-index: 30`). Escape did nothing, so the popover and its full-viewport backdrop stayed mounted; the next `pointerdown` anywhere — including a Library row — hit the backdrop instead (confirmed via `document.elementFromPoint`), and because a drag's mousedown and mouseup both land on that same full-screen element, the native `click` fired there too, running `closePopover()` instead of starting a placement. Fix: one `window` `keydown` listener in `OperationsToolbar.vue`, registered on the **capture** phase so it reliably runs before `TimelineStrips.vue`'s own Escape handler regardless of mount order (that handler already depends on this priority via `event.defaultPrevented`, per its own "close the open popover/dialog/menu, else leave preview" doc comment) — closes `openPopover` and calls `preventDefault()`. Covers all three popover types since they share the one ref/backdrop; confirmed directly for Snap/Align with a new e2e test, not just by inspection. Checked every other popover/dialog in the app (`FileMenu`, `ConflictDialog`, `TimelineStrips`, `ResolutionPopover`) — each already had working Escape handling; this bug was isolated to the Operations toolbar. RED confirmed by `git stash`-ing the fix and re-running the same manual repro (`data-body-count` stuck at 1 after the second drag), then restored. Tests: 2181 unit (unchanged — no core/unit-level surface touched), typecheck clean; targeted new e2e (3 cases: 2 in `fillet-chamfer-operation.e2e.js`, 1 in `snap-align-operation.e2e.js`) 15/15 passed on Chromium+WebKit × all 3 viewports; full non-Firefox matrix **1494 passed / 304 skipped / 0 failed** (777 failures, all Firefox, 259 each of phone/tablet/laptop — the documented pre-existing sandbox launch defect, `TROUBLESHOOTING.md` 2026-09-17, not this task's). An older build ignores the field, meets `user.*` as an unknown op and refuses the file by name, which is the spec's "fails like an unknown op". Registration is global but layered (project beats library); only the store's live document may publish the project layer. A bad or missing part is a named LoadWarning plus a left-broken row (`ui-052`'s machinery), not a stand-in shape and not a refused file. Capture follows `target`/`operands` only, rewrites ids `r1..rn` and self-verifies by `sameGeometry`, so it throws rather than return a part that builds something else. **Step 6**, driven by hand with a real jittered mouse on Chromium and WebKit at laptop width: scenarios 3, 5, 6, 10 PASS; `core-020`, `core-024` and `ui-030` repros PASS; the new capability driven end to end without UI (group, capture in the page, place, autosave reload, File save, open in a fresh browser context with no library) PASS, and a file with a missing part, a disallowed op, a self-containing part and an oversize part each opened with a named warning and a broken row.
